- q_{-m}: r=b: h_a(1-h_b).
Other pairs independent.

Smooth gradient details:
```
def smooth_grad(h, w, n):
    L=2*n-1
    target=n/2
    # weight vector centered length L for lags m=-(n-1)..(n-1)
    w_pad = w  # length L, index n-1+m
    b = 1.0 - h
    A = np.convolve(b, w_pad, mode='full')[n-1:2*n-1]
    B = np.convolve(h, w_pad[::-1], mode='full')[n-1:2*n-1]
    return A - B
```

We can then adjust with a simple bounded mean shift in few iterations: after clip, if sum too high/low, add/subtract only to interior (0,1) with clipping. Define:
```
def project_quick(h,n,target,iters=50):
  h=np.clip(h,0,1)
  for _ in range(iters):
    diff=target-h.sum()
    if abs(diff)<1e-10: break
    mask=(h>0)&(h<1)
    if not mask.any(): # if at bounds, need more advanced: if diff positive, cannot add? But should be possible if target between 0 and n.
       # n/2, so if all 0 or1? target not possible unless n/2=0/n. not.
    h[mask] += diff/mask.sum()
    h=np.clip(h,0,1)
  return h
```
This is previous iterative and can be slow if many saturations but usually few. Could combine: if mean step preserves sum, y sum=target exactly (within rounding), clipping only changes sum slightly; then above converges fast. Good. Use `step = grad - grad.mean()`.

### Proposed solution pipeline

We can focus on **binary optimization** with correct delta, plus continuous smoothing optional. Since target can be met by binary, avoid complicated continuous. But continuous parent may help starting point.

Functions:
- `project_feasible(h,n)`: fall back perhaps not heavily used for binary.
- `compute_c5(h,n)`: returns max correlation * dx.
- `binary_from_fraction(h,n)`: choose top n/2 by h, break ties by index? Use argsort descending; if ties, stable. To get sum exact.
- `corr_full(h)`: `np.correlate(h, 1-h, mode='full')`. Use h float or int. For binary, can use int; correlate int returns int? With h int, 1-h int, yes. But use float to avoid int overflow? Counts <=400, int fine. Use int64 for speed. `corr = np.correlate(h_int, 1-h_int, mode='full')`. But h binary could be int8; correlate may use int. We'll use `h` float? q needed for comparisons, but binary integer faster. We can keep `h` as int8/bool. `corr` int64. For continuous, need float.
- `swap_candidate(h_int, corr, i, j, n, revone)`: returns new_corr and new_max? To avoid repeated tmp allocations, implement:
```
def eval_swap(corr, h, i, j, n):
    tmp = corr.copy()
    tmp[i:n+i] += h_rev_one  # 1 - h[::-1]
    tmp[n-1:2*n-1-i] -= h[i:]
    tmp[j:n+j] -= h_rev_one
    tmp[n-1:2*n-1-j] += h[j:]
    tmp[n-1] -= 2
    tmp[n-1+j-i] += 1
    tmp[n-1+i-j] += 1
    return tmp, int(tmp.max())
```

def smooth_obj_jac(x, n, alpha, use_sum=True, lam=None):
    q = np.correlate(x, 1-x, 'full')
    gm = q.max()
    shifted = alpha*(q-gm)
    # to avoid underflow all zero? exp(shifted) if shifted very negative => 0; if all 0? If q exactly equal maybe all 0 -> sum 1. But due underflow, if gm float and differences > 745/alpha, all other zero; active at least one zero? For max elements shifted=0 if exactly gm; if multiple, exp0. If due rounding gm > some? okay. If sum==0 (all underflow? impossible because max shifted=0). If multiple max but floating gm maybe from argmax, one 0.
    ex = np.exp(shifted)
    Z = ex.sum()
    val = gm + np.log(Z)/alpha
    lam = ex/Z
    # if sum constraint penalty, add rho*(sum-target)^2
    s = x.sum() - n/2.0
    if rho:
       val += rho*s*s
       jac = smooth_grad(x,lam,n) + 2*rho*s*ones
    else: smooth_grad - maybe subtract mean? SLSQP with equality no need.
```

**Feasible projection** after continuous:
```
def project_feasible(h,n):
   h=np.clip(np.asarray(h,float),0,1)
   target=n/2
   for _ in range(100):
      diff=target-h.sum()
      if abs(diff)<1e-12: break
      mask=(h>0)&(h<1)
      if mask.all():
          h += diff
          # but may exceed bounds if diff large? If all interior and total sum adjust, h+diff may exceed if some? all interior but diff could push some outside; use weighted.
      elif mask.any():
          h[mask] += diff/mask.sum()
          h=np.clip(h,0,1)
      else: break
   return h
```
Maybe use bisection for exactness:
```
def project_sum(h,n):
  target=n/2
  lo,hi=-1.0,1.0 # theta in y-theta
  for _ in range(40):
     mid=(lo+hi)/2
     s=np.clip(h-mid,0,1).sum()
     if s > target: lo=mid
     else: hi=mid
  return np.clip(h-(lo+hi)/2,0,1)
```
This ensures box+sum. If h sum target already, 40 ufunc maybe. We can use this after continuous and final.

```
def active_reduce(h,n,deadline,max_passes=50):
  q=corr; cur=q.max(); best...
  while time<deadline and passes:
    targets = indices where q == cur
    # random shuffle targets
    candidates=set()
    for idx in targets[:20]:
       m=idx-(n-1)
       rmin,max ranges
       for r in range(rmin,rmax): if h[r]==0 and h[r+m]==1: candidates.add((r,r+m))
    # To avoid huge set, sample up to 200.
    for (i,j) in candidates shuffled:
       tmp,new=eval_swap
       if new < cur:
          h[i],h[j]=1,0; q=tmp; cur=new; update best; break pass
    if no improve: break after maybe one pass
```

Direct sample function:
```
def random_direct_pair(q,h,n,cur,rng):
  idx = rng.randint(len(q))
  if q[idx] < cur: return None
  m=idx-(n-1)
  r0,r1 valid ranges
  if r1-r0==0 return None
  r = r0 + rng.randint(r1-r0)
  if h[r]==0 and h[r+m]==1: return r, r+m
  else random fallback
```
This focuses improvements.
- For n small, random enough.

def smooth_step_batch(h,n,deadline,step_sizes):
   target = n/2
   best_h=h; best_c=compute_c5
   for alpha in alphas: # maybe [50,200,1000,5000]
      lr = 0.02

      ...

Define:
```
def corr_max_smooth_values(h,n,alpha):
    q=np.correlate(h,1-h,'full')
    gm=q.max()
    shifted=alpha*(q-gm)
    e=np.exp(shifted)
    z=e.sum()
    vals = gm + np.log(z)/alpha
    return vals, e/z
```
But if all shifted underflow except max, z=1, log0. Good.
`smooth_objective(x, n, alpha, rho):` compute q, gm, val. If rho>0 add `(sum-target)^2`.
`smooth_gradient(x, n, alpha, rho):` compute q, gm, ex, z, lam. `grad = smooth_grad(x,lam,n)`. If rho: `grad += 2*rho*(x.sum()-target)*np.ones(n)`.
`smooth_grad(x,lam,n)` via convolve. Need lam length L. x length n. For L large. `np.convolve` of arrays. If lam symmetric? Use formula. Test orientation? For gradient of q_m = ∑ h_{i+m}(1-h_i) under our orientation. Need ensure grad aligns with lam indexing (lag m). We derived A/B using m. Let's test with small n=2, h=[0,1], lam for m=1 only [0,0,1]. q_m m1=1. Gradient wrt h: m1 term h1(1-h0): d/dh1=1, d/dh0=-1. So grad [-1,1]. Our smooth_grad formula: lam for m=1 => w_pad index? m=1 at t=2 [0,0,1]. b=1-h=[1,0]. A = convolve(b, w_pad)[n-1:n-1+n] slice [1:3]. convolve [1,0] and [0,0,1] = [0,0,1]; slice [0,1]? Wait output length4? b len2, w len3 -> [0,0,1? Let's compute: y0=1*0=0; y1=1*0+0*0=0; y2=1*1+0*0=1; y3=0*1=0. slice index n-1=1 to 3 exclusive => [0,1]. A=[0,1]. B = convolve(h, w_pad[::-1] [1,0,0]) output: h [0,1] conv [1,0,0] = [0,1,0,0]; slice [1:3] = [1,0]. grad=[-1,1]. Good. ```
def project_quick(h,n,target):
    h=np.clip(h,0,1)
    for _ in range(50):
        diff=target-h.sum()
        if abs(diff)<1e-10: break
        mask=(h>0)&(h<1)
        if not mask.any(): break
        h[mask] += diff / mask.count
        if (h<0).any() or (h>1).any(): h=np.clip(h,0,1)
    return h
```
Works if few saturations. For gradient step, okay.

Now candidate evaluation function:
```
def swap_eval(corr, h, h_rev_one, i, j, n):
    tmp = corr.copy()
    tmp[i:n+i] += h_rev_one
    tmp[n-1:2*n-1-i] -= h[i:]
    tmp[j:n+j] -= h_rev_one
    tmp[n-1:2*n-1-j] += h[j:]
    tmp[n-1] -= 2
    tmp[n-1+j-i] += 1
    tmp[n-1+i-j] += 1
    m = tmp.max()
    return tmp, int(m)
```
Potential issue: `h[i:]` length n-i; `tmp[n-1:2n-1-i]` length n-i; okay. But `h[i:]` is view; if h int64. Good.
We can make a faster version that doesn't return tmp if newmax not better? It always returns. It returns tmp; if reject, tmp discarded. Fine.

Steepest pass:
```
def steepest_pass(h, corr, cur, n, deadline, seed):
    zeros = np.where(h==0)[0]
    ones = np.where(h==1)[0]
    h_rev_one = 1 - h[::-1]
    bestnew=cur; best_i=-1; best_j=-1; best_tmp=None
    total = len(zeros)*len(ones)
    count=0
    for i in zeros:
      for j in ones:
         count+=1
         if (count & 1023)==0 and time.time()>deadline: return h,corr,cur,best...
         tmp, newmax = swap_eval(...)
         if newmax < bestnew:
             bestnew=newmax; best_i=i; best_j=j; best_tmp=tmp
             # if bestnew == lower_bound? break
    if best_i>=0 and bestnew < cur:
       h[best_i]=1; h[best_j]=0; corr=best_tmp; cur=bestnew
    return h,corr,cur
```
If multiple equal, first. If bestnew == cur, no accept. This is worst-case O(nz*no*O(n)). For n=100, okay. For n=200, maybe. Use only n<=200.

Detailed smooth_refine:
```
def smooth_refine(h, n, deadline, alphas=(50,200,1000,5000)):
    target=n/2.0
    best_h=h.copy(); best_c=compute_c5(h,n)
    for alpha in alphas:
       if time.time()>deadline-2: break
       h=best_h.copy()? maybe continue from current h (best or current)
       lr=0.05
       for it in range(100000):
          if time.time()>deadline-1: break
          q=np.correlate(h,1-h,'full')
          gm=float(q.max())
          # F for acceptance
          shifted=alpha*(q-gm)
          ex=np.exp(shifted); z=ex.sum()
          # if z==0: ex=np.ones_like(q)/len
          F=gm + np.log(z)/alpha
          lam=ex/z
          grad=smooth_grad(h,lam,n)
          grad -= grad.mean()
          accepted=False
          for factor in [1.0,0.5,0.25,0.1]:
              y=h - lr*factor*grad
              hnew=project_quick(y,n)
              qn=np.correlate(hnew,1-hnew,'full'); gmn=float(qn.max())
              shiftedn=alpha*(qn-gmn); expn=np.exp(shiftedn); zn=expn.sum(); Fn=gmn+np.log(zn)/alpha
              if Fn <= F + 1e-10 or gmn*dx? (we minimize counts) maybe if gmn < gm:
                 h=hnew; q=qn; gm=gmn; F=Fn; accepted=True; break
          if not accepted:
             # reduce lr
             lr *= 0.5
             if lr < 1e-5: break
          c = compute_c5(h,n)
          if c<best_c: best_c=c; best_h=h.copy()
       # maybe reset lr = 0.05/sqrt(alpha)?
    return best_h,best_c
```

**Direct active candidate generation in anneal**:
```
def direct_candidate(h, q, cur, n, rng):
   # try up to 5 random active indices
   for _ in range(5):
       idx = int(rng.randint(len(q)))
       if q[idx] != cur: continue
       m = idx - (n - 1)
       if m >= 0:
           r0=0; r1=n-m
       else:
           r0=-m; r1=n
       if r1>r0:
          r = int(r0 + rng.randint(r1-r0))
          if h[r]==0 and h[r+m]==1:
             return (r, r+m)
   return None
```
Use this 50% in anneal.

try: from scipy.optimize import minimize # maybe not used

def compute_c5(h,n):
    return float(np.max(np.correlate(h, 1-h, mode='full')) * (2.0/n))

def project_feasible(h,n):
    h=np.clip(np.asarray(h,float),0,1)
    target=n/2.0
    lo,hi=-1.0,1.0
    for _ in range(60):
       mid=(lo+hi)*0.5
       s=np.clip(h-mid,0,1).sum()
       if s>target: lo=mid
       else: hi=mid
    h=np.clip(h-(lo+hi)*0.5,0,1)
    # adjust tiny residual
    diff=target-h.sum()
    if abs(diff)>1e-12:
       h += diff
    return np.clip(h,0,1)
```

`project_quick`:
```
def project_quick(h,n,target=None):
    if target is None: target=n/2.0
    h=np.clip(h,0.1? no 0,1)
    for _ in range(30):
       diff=target-h.sum()
       if abs(diff)<1e-11: break
       mask=(h>0)&(h<1)
       cnt=mask.sum()
       if cnt==0:
          # use bisection fallback
          return project_feasible(h,n)
       h[mask]+=diff/cnt
       h=np.clip(h,0,1)
    return h
```
If diff large and many saturate, loops more than 30; fallback. Good.

Smooth refine:
```
def smooth_refine(h,n,deadline,alphas=(30,200,1500,8000)):
    target=n/2.0
    best_h=np.asarray(h,float).copy()
    best_c=compute_c5(best_h,n)
    dx=2.0/n
    for alpha in alphas:
       h=best_h.copy() # or current? Use best to avoid bad drift
       lr=0.05/(1.0+alpha/100.0)
       best_local_c=best_c
       for it in range(1000000):
          if time.time()>deadline-2: break
          q=np.correlate(h,1-h,'full')
          gm=q.max()
          shifted=alpha*(q-gm)
          ex=np.exp(shifted); z=ex.sum()
          if z<=0: ex=np.ones_like(q)/len(q); z=len(q)
          lam=ex/z
          grad=smooth_grad(h,lam,n)
          grad -= grad.mean()
          # If grad zero: break
          if np.max(np.abs(grad))<1e-13: break
          accepted=False
          for fac in (1.0,0.5,0.25,0.1):
              y=h - lr*fac*grad
              hnew=project_quick(y,n)
              qn=np.correlate(hnew,1-hnew,'full')
              gmn=qn.max()
              # accept if qmax decreased or F (optional)
              # compute F? Maybe only if gm close. Use smoothed F to allow local.
              shiftedn=alpha*(qn-gmn); exn=np.exp(shiftedn); zn=exn.sum()
              F = gm + math.log(z)/alpha
              Fn = gmn + math.log(zn)/alpha
              if Fn <= F + 1e-9 or gmn*dx < best_local_c:
                 h=hnew; q=qn; gm=gmn; accepted=True; c=compute_c5? use gm*dx
                 if gm*dx < best_local_c:
                     best_local_c=gm*dx; best_h=h.copy()
                 break
          if not accepted:
             lr *= 0.5
             if lr < 1e-6: break
          # annealing: maybe accept even if F increases slightly? we can modify condition to Fn <= F+T
       # after alpha, if best_local < best_c update global? we already update best_h
    return best_h,best_c
```
This recomputes F with `z` from previous q, but if accepted, q updated; good. The acceptance condition `gmn*dx < best_local_c` uses global best, might accept steps that improve max even if F increases; good. However after accepting, next loop recomputes q.
Note: `F` uses z from current q; if z huge? exp shifted sum, log could large but fine. `math.log(z)` where z float. If z <1? exp shifted includes at least one 1 so z>=1. Good.
This gradient descent might be very slow due manyproj quick bisection. But optional; if no improvement, fine. Use small alphas and limited iterations. Could it ruin best_h? We only update best_h on improvement, and h local may drift but next alpha restarts from best_h. Good.

    rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
    h=make_binary(frac,n).astype(np.int64)

Let's refine `steepest_pass` code for performance:
```
def steepest_pass(h, q, cur, n, deadline):
    zeros = np.where(h == 0)[0]
    ones = np.where(h == 1)[0]
    hrev = 1 - h[::-1]
    bestnew = cur
    bi = bj = -1
    bt = None
    count = 0
    for i in zeros:
        if time.time() > deadline:
            break
        hi = h[i:]
        # Can't pre-slice term2? For each i, term2 slice uses h[i:] and start n-1. Term1 uses hrev to tmp[i:n+i]
        for j in ones:
            count += 1
            if (count & 1023) == 0 and time.time() > deadline:
                break
            tmp = q.copy()
            tmp[i:n+i] += hrev
            tmp[n-1:2*n-1-i] -= hi
            tmp[j:n+j] -= hrev
            tmp[n-1:2*n-1-j] += h[j:]
            tmp[n-1] -= 2
            tmp[n-1+j-i] += 1
            tmp[n-1+i-j] += 1
            nm = int(tmp.max())
            if nm < bestnew:
                bestnew = nm; bi=i; bj=j; bt=tmp
        if (count & 4095)==0 and time.time()>deadline: break
    if bi >= 0 and bestnew < cur:
        h[bi]=1; h[bj]=0; q=bt; cur=bestnew
        return h,q,cur,True
    return h,q,cur,False
```
Potential bug: `tmp = q.copy(); tmp[i:n+i] += hrev` where hrev is full reversed (1-h). For term1 with arbitrary i, we add hrev to slice starting i length n. Is this always correct? We derived term1 adds (1-h[p]) at lag m=i-p, p ranges 0..n-1. The values for t start i to i+n-1 correspond p =? Let's re-derive term1 slice by t. t = n-1 + m = n-1+i-p. For p=n-1 down to 0, t = i to n+i-1. Values (1-h[p]) in order p=n-1,n-2,...,0 = (1-h[::-1][::-1]? Wait h[::-1] order p=n-1 down to 0. Yes `hrev = 1 - h[::-1]` order p=n-1..0. So `tmp[i:n+i] += hrev` correct. For i=0, slice 0:n values h_{n-1}..h0; p=n-1..0, t=p? t=n-1-p? Wait t=n-1+i-p = n-1-p. For p=n-1, t=0; p=0, t=n-1. Slice indices 0..n-1, values hrev[0]=1-h[n-1], hrev[n-1]=1-h0. Correct.
Term2: p=i+m, p=i..n-1, m=p-i, t=n-1+p-i. Slice start n-1 end n-1+n-i = 2n-1-i. Values h[i]..h[n-1] forward. Good.
Term3 same hrev to slice j:n+j negative. Good.
Term4 h[j:] to slice n-1:2n-1-j.

`direct_candidate` for anneal:
```
def random_direct(h,q,cur,n,rng):
    for _ in range(4):
        idx=int(rng.randint(len(q)))
        if q[idx] != cur: continue
        m=idx-(n-1)
        if m>=0: r0=0; r1=n-m
        else: r0=-m; r1=n
        if r1>r0:
            r=int(r0+rng.randint(r1-r0))
            if h[r]==0 and h[r+m]==1: return r, r+m
    return None
```
Note if m negative, r+m may be less than 0? Range ensures valid.

def make_binary(frac,n):

    k=n//2
    # use argpartition for speed but top exact? Use argsort stable.

def resample_h(src, n, target_n):
    if len(src)==target_n: return src.copy()
    xs=np.linspace(0,2,len(src)); xn=np.linspace(0,2,target_n)
    return np.interp(xn,xs,src)
```

Detailed code:

```
import numpy as np
import time
import math

def compute_c5(h,n):
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode='full')
    return float(np.max(c) * (2.0 / n))
```
If h int, `1.0-h` float? If we use `1.0`, corr float. For binary local q we want int; use a separate `corr_counts`:
```
def corr_counts(h):
    return np.correlate(h, 1 - h, mode='full').astype(np.int64, copy=False)
```
If h int64, 1-h int64; astype int64. For continuous compute use float.

`project_feasible`:
```
def project_feasible(h,n):
    h = np.clip(np.array(h, dtype=float), 0.0, 1.0)
    target = n / 2.0
    lo, hi = -1.0, 1.0
    for _ in range(70):
        mid = 0.5*(lo+hi)
        if np.clip(h - mid, 0.0, 1.0).sum() > target:
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid
    h = np.clip(h - 0.5*(lo+hi), 0.0, 1.0)
    h += (target - h.sum()) / max(1,n)
    return np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
```
The final uniform add may break bounds by tiny; clip. Good.

`project_quick`:
```
def project_quick(h,n):
    target=n/2.0
    h=np.clip(h,0.0,1.0)
    for _ in range(60):
       diff=target-h.sum()
       if abs(diff)<1e-11: return h
       mask=(h>0.0)&(h<1.0)
       cnt=mask.sum()
       if cnt==0:
           return project_feasible(h,n)
       h[mask] += diff/cnt
       if (h<0.0).any() or (h>1.0).any():
           h=np.clip(h,0.0,1.0)
    return h
```

`smooth_grad`:
```
def smooth_grad(x,w,n):
    b=1.0-x
    A=np.convolve(b,w,mode='full')[n-1:2*n-1]
    B=np.convolve(x,w[::-1],mode='full')[n-1:2*n-1]
    return A-B
```
If x list? use `x=np.asarray(x,float)`. w array.

`smooth_refine` as above, with try/except and time checks. Need be careful using `z` from before after maybe q not updated? We'll recompute each iteration. To compute F, use `math.log(z)`; if z is numpy scalar, ok. We can skip F for speed, only accept if `gmn < gm or (gmn*dx >= best_c? and Fn <= F + ...)`. Need F. Could simplify: accept if `gmn < gm` or `Fn < F`. Good.
```
def smooth_refine(h,n,deadline):
    try:
      best_h=np.array(h,float); best_c=compute_c5(best_h,n); dx=2.0/n
      alphas=(20.0,120.0,800.0,5000.0)
      for alpha in alphas:
         if time.time()>deadline-3: break
         h=best_h.copy()
         lr=0.04/(1.0+0.01*alpha)
         for it in range(200000):
            if time.time()>deadline-2: break
            q=np.correlate(h,1.0-h,'full')
            gm=float(q.max())
            if gm<=1e-12: break
            shifted=alpha*(q-gm)
            ex=np.exp(shifted); z=float(ex.sum())
            if z<=0.0:
                ex=np.ones_like(q,dtype=float); z=float(len(q))
            lam=ex/z
            grad=smooth_grad(h,lam,n)
            grad -= grad.mean()
            step_norm=float(np.max(np.abs(grad)))
            if step_norm<1e-13: break
            F=gm + math.log(z)/alpha
            accepted=False
            for fac in (1.0,0.5,0.25,0.1):
                hp=project_quick(h-lr*fac*grad,n)
                qn=np.correlate(hp,1.0-hp,'full')
                gmn=float(qn.max())
                shiftedn=alpha*(qn-gmn)
                exn=np.exp(shiftedn); zn=float(exn.sum())
                if zn<=0.0: continue
                Fn=gmn + math.log(zn)/alpha
                if Fn <= F + 1e-10 or gmn*dx < best_c:
                    h=hp; q=qn; gm=gmn; F=Fn; accepted=True
                    if gmn*dx < best_c:
                       best_c=gmn*dx; best_h=h.copy()
                    break
            if not accepted:
                lr *= 0.5
                if lr < 1e-7: break
    except Exception:
        pass
    return project_feasible(best_h,n), float(best_c)
```
This may be slow capturing q after accept; not recompute F until next. Fine.
But `if gmn*dx < best_c`: best_c current global; if reducing max but F increases, accept. If best_h updated, next alpha restart from best. Good. If accepted but c not improve, h can drift; if line search not accept, lr reduces; may still no improvement and break. Good.
Potential `z` nan if q contains nan? no.

**make_binary**:
```
def make_binary(frac,n):
    h=np.zeros(n,dtype=np.int64)
    if n>0:
       k=n//2
       if k>0:
          idx=np.argsort(np.asarray(frac,dtype=float))[::-1][:k]
          h[idx]=1
    return h
```
For n=0 no.

`lower_bound_count`:
```
def lower_bound_count(n):
    total=(n//2)*(n//2)
    L=2*n-2
    if L<=0: return 0
    return int((total+L-1)//L)
```

`swap_eval` top-level with local constants:
```
def swap_eval(q,h,hrev,i,j,n):
    c=n-1; L=2*n-1
    tmp=q.copy()
    tmp[i:n+i] += hrev
    tmp[c:L-i] -= h[i:]  # L-i = 2n-1-i, correct
    tmp[j:n+j] -= hrev
    tmp[c:L-j] += h[j:]
    tmp[c] -= 2
    tmp[c+j-i] += 1
    tmp[c+i-j] += 1
    return tmp, int(tmp.max())
```
Check `L-i`: if i=0, L=2n-1, end 2n-1 length n; if i=n-1, end n, length1. Good.
`tmp[c+j-i]` index safe. If j-i int. Good.
Note: `tmp[i:n+i] += hrev` length n? When i=n-1, end 2n-1 = L, start n-1 length n? Wait length = n+i - i = n. But slice start c? For i=n-1, start n-1 end 2n-1 length n, values hrev full. Is term1 for i=n-1 valid all lags? Yes p 0..n-1, m=i-p ranges n-1 down to0, t n-1..2n-2. Start i=n-1, end 2n-1 length n. Good.

`steepest_pass` as above. Need when timeout inside inner, break outer. Use flag.
```
def steepest_pass(h,q,cur,n,deadline):
    zeros = np.where(h == 0)[0]
    ones = np.where(h == 1)[0]
    hrev = 1 - h[::-1]
    c=n-1; L=2*n-1
    bestnew=cur; bi=-1; bj=-1; bt=None
    checked=0
    for i in zeros:
        hi = h[i:]
        for j in ones:
            checked +=1
            if (checked & 1024)==0 and time.time()>deadline:
                break
            tmp = q.copy()
            tmp[i:n+i] += hrev
            tmp[c:L-i] -= hi
            tmp[j:n+j] -= hrev
            tmp[c:L-j] += h[j:]
            tmp[c] -= 2
            tmp[c+j-i] += 1
            tmp[c+i-j] += 1
            nm=int(tmp.max())
            if nm < bestnew:
                bestnew=nm; bi=i; bj=j; bt=tmp
        if (checked & 4096)==0 and time.time()>deadline:
            break
    if bi>=0 and bestnew<cur:
        h[bi]=1; h[bj]=0; q=bt; cur=bestnew
        return h,q,cur,True
    return h,q,cur,False
```
Bug: If `time.time()>deadline` after a pass, but it already found best, still accept. Good.
Could be very slow for n=200 if many passes and no improve. If no improve in one full pass, outer while breaks. We'll implement in optimize: after steepest_pass returns changed False, break to anneal. Good.

`active_pass`:
```
def active_pass(h,q,cur,n,deadline,rng):
    idxs = np.where(q == cur)[0]
    if idxs.size == 0: return h,q,cur,False
    if idxs.size > 40:
        sample = idxs[rng.choice(idxs.size, 40, replace=False)]
    else:
        sample = idxs.copy(); rng.shuffle(sample)
    cand=[]; seen=set()
    L=2*n-1; c=n-1
    limit = 800 if n <= 200 else 500
    for idx in sample:
        m=idx-c
        if m>=0: r0=0; r1=n-m
        else: r0=-m; r1=n
        for r in range(r0,r1):
            if h[r]==0 and h[r+m]==1:
                pair=r*n + (r+m)
                if pair not in seen:
                    seen.add(pair); cand.append((r,r+m))
                    if len(cand)>=limit: break
        if len(cand)>=limit: break
    if not cand: return h,q,cur,False
    # Optional random shuffle candidates
    rng.shuffle(cand) if cand is list? `rng.shuffle` doesn't work on list? It does for sequence? `rng.shuffle(cand)` works on list? Numpy RandomState.shuffle expects array-like, can shuffle list in place? It may convert? Better: `order = rng.permutation(len(cand)); cand = [cand[k] for k in order]`.
    hrev = 1 - h[::-1]
    for i,j in cand:
        if time.time()>deadline: break
        tmp = q.copy(); ... eval
        nm=int(tmp.max())
        if nm < cur:
            h[i]=1; h[j]=0; q=tmp; cur=nm
            return h,q,cur,True
    return h,q,cur,False
```
`pair=r*n + (r+m)` where r+m can be negative? But range ensures 0..n-1. Unique. If i zero j one. Good.

`optimize_binary`:
```
def optimize_binary(frac,n,deadline,seed):
    rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
    h=make_binary(frac,n)
    q=np.correlate(h,1-h,'full').astype(np.int64)
    cur=int(q.max())
    lb=lower_bound_count(n)
    best_h=h.copy(); best_q=cur
    t_start=time.time()
    phaseA = min(deadline, t_start + 0.45*(deadline-t_start))
    if n <= 220:
        while time.time() < phaseA and cur > lb:
            h,q,cur,changed = steepest_pass(h,q,cur,n, min(deadline, time.time()+30?))
            # steepest uses its own deadline; if changed, update best
            if cur < best_q: best_q=cur; best_h=h.copy()
            if not changed: break
        # If phaseA still and improved? Could do another if time. changed false break. steepest pass one acceptance per pass; after phase maybe if cur improved and changed true but no more pass due time.
    else:
        while time.time() < phaseA and cur > lb:
            h,q,cur,changed=active_pass(h,q,cur,n, min(deadline, time.time()+10), rng)
            if cur<best_q: best_q=cur; best_h=h.copy()
            if not changed: break
    # anneal from current best_h or current h? Use current h if not worse? Since anneal can escape; use h (current) but track best. If current worse than best due accepted worsen in previous? Anneal not started yet. steepest/active only accept improvements, so current=best. use h.
    zeros=np.where(h==0)[0]; ones=np.where(h==1)[0]
    hrev=1-h[::-1]
    T = 2.0 if n <= 200 else 3.0
    Tmin=0.02; cool=0.99995
    it=0
    while time.time() < deadline:
        it +=1
        # direct?
        i=j=-1
        if rng.random() < 0.45:
            cand=random_direct(h,q,cur,n,rng)
            if cand is not None: i,j=cand
        if i < 0:
            # random
            zi=int(rng.randint(len(zeros))); oi=int(rng.randint(len(ones)))
            i=int(zeros[zi]); j=int(ones[oi])
        # Need ensure h[i]==0,h[j]==1 after direct? direct returns; random arrays valid.
        tmp,nm=swap_eval(q,h,hrev,i,j,n)
        accept = nm < cur
        if not accept and T > 1e-6:
            d = cur - nm
            if d > -8.0 * T:  # avoid exp too negative
                accept = (math.exp(d / T) > rng.random())
        if accept:
            h[i]=1; h[j]=0; q=tmp; cur=nm
            if cur < best_q:
                best_q=cur; best_h=h.copy()
            # update arrays
            zeros=np.where(h==0)[0]; ones=np.where(h==1)[0]; hrev=1-h[::-1]
            T=max(Tmin,T*cool)
        else:
            T=max(Tmin,T*cool)
        if (it & 2047)==0 and time.time()>deadline: break
    return best_h, best_q
```
Potential issue: In random fallback, if `zeros` or `ones` empty due bug, no. n even.
When direct candidate returns i,j, h[i] zero h[j] one; but if we don't update zeros/ones arrays, after direct accept we rebuild. Good.
If direct candidate returns but `hrev` stale? h unchanged since last rebuild, okay.
Cooling every iter; if many rejections, T cools to Tmin quickly; then only improvements. Maybe too fast for long? n=100, 1M iter factor 0.99995 -> 0.0067 from 2 to 0.013, okay. For 10M iter -> underflow. Time likely <1M for small n? For n=100, 10k/s*300s=3M, factor 0.00006, Tmin. Good.
If T too low, stuck; but best kept.

**Active pass deadline**: If `time.time()+10` too long? active pass may scan many; but 10s max. Good. If phaseA remaining less than 10, min.
**Steepest pass deadline**: It might use entire remaining (seconds). Could check. For n=220, pass ~0.5s, okay. If many passes, allocate.
Maybe use `pass_dl = min(phaseA, time.time()+max(5, 0.1*(phaseA-time.time())))`? For steepest, a single pass doesn't take that long. Use pass_dl=phaseA is okay; it checks and may break early if timeout. If n=220 and Python slow, one pass could be 10s; still within phase. Good.

**Best global update for binary**:
```
def better_count_update(h_int,qcount,n):
    c = float(qcount) * (2.0/n)
    if c < best_c - 1e-15:
       best_c = c; best_n = n; best_h = h_int.astype(float)
```

So derivation of term slices still wrong. Let's derive carefully again. We need exact delta for support linear correlation q_m = ∑_r h_{r+m} (1-h_r). Let's use bilinear expansion with correct first-order terms.
Define q = corr(A,B), A=h, B=1-h. Using orientation: q_m = ∑_r A_{r+m} B_r. Let indices integer.
Change: i 0->1 (A_i +1, B_i -1), j 1->0 (A_j -1, B_j +1).
Exact delta from linear + second:
Change in A: δA = e_i - e_j.
Change in B: δB = -e_i + e_j.
q' - q = corr(δA, B) + corr(A, δB) + corr(δA, δB).
Where corr(X,Y)_m = ∑_r X_{r+m} Y_r.
Compute corr(e_p, B)_m: X has 1 at p: nonzero when r+m = p => r = p-m; value B_{p-m}. So term + B_{p-m} at lag m if p-m valid.
Compute corr(A, e_p)_m: Y has 1 at p: r=p; value A_{p+m} at lag m if p+m valid.
Thus first-order for i: +B_{i-m} - A_{i+m}.
For j (δA=-e_j, δB=+e_j): -corr(e_j,B) + corr(A,e_j) = -B_{j-m} + A_{j+m}.
So S[m] = B_{i-m} - A_{i+m} - B_{j-m} + A_{j+m}. With B=1-A.
Second-order corr(δA,δB):
- i with -i: δA e_i, δB -e_i => - corr(e_i,e_i) = -1 at m=0.
- -j with +j: -1 at m=0.
- e_i with +e_j: + corr(e_i,e_j): X e_i (A), Y e_j (B), nonzero when r=j, r+m=i -> m=i-j. +1 at m=i-j.
- -e_j with -e_i: (+1) corr(e_j,e_i): r=i, r+m=j -> m=j-i. +1.

def swap_eval(q,h,hrev,i,j,n):
    c=n-1
    tmp=q.copy()
    tmp[i:n+i] += hrev
    tmp[c-i:n+c-i] -= h  # c-i+n
    tmp[j:n+j] -= hrev
    tmp[c-j:n+c-j] += h
    tmp[c] -= 2
    tmp[c+i-j] += 1
    tmp[c+j-i] += 1
    return tmp, int(tmp.max())

```python
import numpy as np
import time
import math


def compute_c5(h, n):
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")
    return float(np.max(c) * (2.0 / n))


def project_feasible(h, n):
    h = np.clip(np.asarray(h, dtype=float), 0.0, 1.0)
    target = n / 2.0
    lo, hi = -1.0, 1.0
    for _ in range(70):
        mid = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
        if np.clip(h - mid, 0.0, 1.0).sum() > target:
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid
    h = np.clip(h - 0.5 * (lo + hi), 0.0, 1.0)
    h += (target - h.sum()) / max(1, n)
    return np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)


def project_quick(h, n):
    target = n / 2.0
    h = np.clip(np.asarray(h, dtype=float), 0.0, 1.0)
    for _ in range(60):
        diff = target - h.sum()
        if abs(diff) < 1e-11:
            return h
        mask = (h > 0.0) & (h < 1.0)
        cnt = mask.sum()
        if cnt == 0:
            return project_feasible(h, n)
        h[mask] += diff / cnt
        if (h < 0.0).any() or (h > 1.0).any():
            h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    return h


def add_n_to_list(n, lst):
    try:
        n = int(n)
    except Exception:
        return
    if n < 40:
        n = 40
    if n > 1000:
        n = 1000
    if n % 2:
        n += 1
    if n > 1000:
        n -= 1
    if 40 <= n <= 1000 and n not in lst:
        lst.append(n)


def resample_h(src, n_src, n_tgt):
    src = np.asarray(src, dtype=float)
    if n_src == n_tgt:
        return src.copy()
    xs = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n_src)
    xn = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n_tgt)
    return np.interp(xn, xs, src)


def lower_bound_count(n):
    total = (n // 2) * (n // 2)
    L = 2 * n - 2
    if L <= 0:
        return 0
    return int((total + L - 1) // L)


def make_binary(frac, n):
    h = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.int64)
    if n > 0:
        k = n // 2
        if k > 0:
            idx = np.argsort(np.asarray(frac, dtype=float))[::-1][:k]
            h[idx] = 1
    return h


def swap_eval(q, h, hrev, i, j, n):
    c = n - 1
    tmp = q.copy()
    tmp[i:n + i] += hrev
    tmp[c - i:n + c - i] -= h
    tmp[j:n + j] -= hrev
    tmp[c - j:n + c - j] += h
    tmp[c] -= 2
    tmp[c + i - j] += 1
    tmp[c + j - i] += 1
    return tmp, int(tmp.max())


def steepest_pass(h, q, cur, n, deadline):
    zeros = np.where(h == 0)[0]
    ones = np.where(h == 1)[0]
    hrev = 1 - h[::-1]
    bestnew = cur
    bi = bj = -1
    bt = None
    checked = 0
    for i in zeros:
        i = int(i)
        for j in ones:
            checked += 1
            if (checked & 1024) == 0 and time.time() > deadline:
                break
            j = int(j)
            tmp, nm = swap_eval(q, h, hrev, i, j, n)
            if nm < bestnew:
                bestnew = nm
                bi, bj, bt = i, j, tmp
        if (checked & 4096) == 0 and time.time() > deadline:
            break
    if bi >= 0 and bestnew < cur:
        h[bi] = 1
        h[bj] = 0
        return h, bt, bestnew, True
    return h, q, cur, False


def active_pass(h, q, cur, n, deadline, rng):
    idxs = np.where(q == cur)[0]
    if idxs.size == 0:
        return h, q, cur, False
    if idxs.size > 40:
        sample = idxs[rng.choice(idxs.size, 40, replace=False)]
    else:
        sample = idxs.copy()
        rng.shuffle(sample)

    cand = []
    seen = set()
    c = n - 1
    limit = 800 if n <= 200 else 500

    for idx in sample:
        idx = int(idx)
        m = idx - c
        if m >= 0:
            r0, r1 = 0, n - m
        else:
            r0, r1 = -m, n
        for r in range(r0, r1):
            if h[r] == 0 and h[r + m] == 1:
                key = r * n + (r + m)
                if key not in seen:
                    seen.add(key)
                    cand.append((r, r + m))
                    if len(cand) >= limit:
                        break
        if len(cand) >= limit:
            break

    if not cand:
        return h, q, cur, False

    if len(cand) > 1:
        order = rng.permutation(len(cand))
        cand = [cand[k] for k in order]

    hrev = 1 - h[::-1]
    for i, j in cand:
        if time.time() > deadline:
            break
        tmp, nm = swap_eval(q, h, hrev, int(i), int(j), n)
        if nm < cur:
            h[int(i)] = 1
            h[int(j)] = 0
            return h, tmp, nm, True
    return h, q, cur, False


def random_direct(h, q, cur, n, rng):
    c = n - 1
    for _ in range(5):
        idx = int(rng.randint(len(q)))
        if q[idx] != cur:
            continue
        m = idx - c
        if m >= 0:
            r0, r1 = 0, n - m
        else:
            r0, r1 = -m, n
        if r1 > r0:
            r = int(r0 + rng.randint(r1 - r0))
            if h[r] == 0 and h[r + m] == 1:
                return r, r + m
    return None


def optimize_binary(frac, n, deadline, seed):
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)
    h = make_binary(frac, n)
    q = np.correlate(h, 1 - h, mode="full").astype(np.int64, copy=False)
    cur = int(q.max())
    lb = lower_bound_count(n)
    best_h = h.copy()
    best_q = cur

    t_start = time.time()
    phase_a = min(deadline, t_start + 0.45 * max(1.0, deadline - t_start))

    if n <= 220:
        while time.time() < phase_a and cur > lb:
            h, q, cur, changed = steepest_pass(h, q, cur, n, phase_a)
            if cur < best_q:
                best_q = cur
                best_h = h.copy()
            if not changed:
                break
    else:
        while time.time() < phase_a and cur > lb:
            dl = min(phase_a, time.time() + 12.0)
            h, q, cur, changed = active_pass(h, q, cur, n, dl, rng)
            if cur < best_q:
                best_q = cur
                best_h = h.copy()
            if not changed:
                break

    zeros = np.where(h == 0)[0]
    ones = np.where(h == 1)[0]
    hrev = 1 - h[::-1]
    T = 2.0 if n <= 200 else 3.0
    Tmin = 0.02
    cool = 0.99995
    it = 0

    while time.time() < deadline:
        it += 1
        i = j = -1
        if rng.random() < 0.45:
            cand = random_direct(h, q, cur, n, rng)
            if cand is not None:
                i, j = cand
        if i < 0:
            zi = int(rng.randint(len(zeros)))
            oi = int(rng.randint(len(ones)))
            i = int(zeros[zi])
            j = int(ones[oi])

        tmp, nm = swap_eval(q, h, hrev, i, j, n)
        accept = nm < cur
        if not accept and T > 1e-6:
            d = cur - nm
            if d > -8.0 * T:
                accept = math.exp(d / T) > rng.random()

        if accept:
            h[i] = 1
            h[j] = 0
            q = tmp
            cur = nm
            if cur < best_q:
                best_q = cur
                best_h = h.copy()
            zeros = np.where(h == 0)[0]
            ones = np.where(h == 1)[0]
            hrev = 1 - h[::-1]

        T = max(Tmin, T * cool)
        if (it & 2047) == 0 and time.time() > deadline:
            break

    return best_h, best_q


def smooth_grad(x, w, n):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=float)
    b = 1.0 - x
    A = np.convolve(b, w, mode="full")[n - 1:2 * n - 1]
    B = np.convolve(x, w[::-1], mode="full")[n - 1:2 * n - 1]
    return A - B


def smooth_refine(h, n, deadline):
    try:
        best_h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float).copy()
        best_c = compute_c5(best_h, n)
        dx = 2.0 / n
        alphas = (20.0, 120.0, 800.0, 5000.0)
        for alpha in alphas:
            if time.time() > deadline - 3.0:
                break
            hh = best_h.copy()
            lr = 0.04 / (1.0 + 0.01 * alpha)
            for _ in range(200000):
                if time.time() > deadline - 2.0:
                    break
                q = np.correlate(hh, 1.0 - hh, mode="full")
                gm = float(q.max())
                if gm <= 1e-12:
                    break
                shifted = alpha * (q - gm)
                ex = np.exp(shifted)
                z = float(ex.sum())
                if z <= 0.0:
                    ex = np.ones_like(q, dtype=float)
                    z = float(len(q))
                lam = ex / z
                grad = smooth_grad(hh, lam, n)
                grad -= grad.mean()
                if float(np.max(np.abs(grad))) < 1e-13:
                    break
                F = gm + math.log(z) / alpha
                accepted = False
                for fac in (1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.1):
                    nh = project_quick(hh - lr * fac * grad, n)
                    qn = np.correlate(nh, 1.0 - nh, mode="full")
                    gmn = float(qn.max())
                    shiftedn = alpha * (qn - gmn)
                    exn = np.exp(shiftedn)
                    zn = float(exn.sum())
                    if zn <= 0.0:
                        continue
                    Fn = gmn + math.log(zn) / alpha
                    if Fn <= F + 1e-10 or gmn * dx < best_c:
                        hh = nh
                        gm = gmn
                        F = Fn
                        accepted = True
                        if gmn * dx < best_c:
                            best_c = gmn * dx
                            best_h = hh.copy()
                        break
                if not accepted:
                    lr *= 0.5
                    if lr < 1e-7:
                        break
        return project_feasible(best_h, n), float(best_c)
    except Exception:
        return project_feasible(best_h, n), float(best_c)


def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.time()
    total = max(60.0, float(budget_s) - 10.0)
    dl = t0 + total

    try:
        hraw = np.asarray(initial_h_values, dtype=float).copy()
        n0 = int(len(hraw))
    except Exception:
        n0 = 200
        hraw = np.random.RandomState(seed).rand(n0)

    if n0 % 2:
        n0e = n0 + 1 if n0 < 1000 else n0 - 1
        hraw = resample_h(hraw, n0, n0e)
        n0 = int(n0e)

    best_h = project_feasible(hraw, n0)
    best_n = n0
    best_c = compute_c5(best_h, best_n)

    if dl - t0 > 160.0 and best_n <= 700:
        sm_dl = t0 + min(70.0, 0.08 * total)
        h_s, c_s = smooth_refine(best_h, best_n, sm_dl)
        if c_s < best_c:
            best_c = c_s
            best_h = h_s
            best_n = len(h_s)

    attempts = []
    add_n_to_list(best_n, attempts)
    add_n_to_list(best_n // 2, attempts)
    for nn in (100, 120, 150, 200, 250, 300):
        add_n_to_list(nn, attempts)

    ordered = []
    if best_n in attempts:
        ordered.append(best_n)
    for nn in sorted(attempts):
        if nn != best_n and nn not in ordered:
            ordered.append(nn)
    attempts = ordered[:6]

    weights = []
    for idx in range(len(attempts)):
        if idx == 0:
            w = 0.22
        elif idx < 3:
            w = 0.20
        elif idx < 5:
            w = 0.13
        else:
            w = 0.10
        weights.append(w)
    sw = sum(weights)
    if sw > 0:
        weights = [w / sw for w in weights]

    rng_main = np.random.RandomState(seed + 101)
    target_c = 0.3808

    for idx, nn in enumerate(attempts):
        if time.time() > dl - 8.0:
            break
        fraction = weights[idx] if idx < len(weights) else 0.1
        if best_c <= target_c:
            fraction *= 0.55
        alloc = max(25.0, min(fraction * (dl - t0), max(0.0, dl - time.time() - 8.0)))
        first_dl = time.time() + alloc

        frac = resample_h(best_h, best_n, nn)
        h_b, q_b = optimize_binary(frac, nn, first_dl, seed + 7 * idx)
        c_b = float(q_b) * (2.0 / nn)
        if c_b < best_c:
            best_c = c_b
            best_n = nn
            best_h = h_b.astype(float).copy()

        rem = dl - time.time()
        if rem > 35.0 and nn <= 300:
            frac2 = 0.55 * frac + 0.45 * rng_main.rand(nn)
            frac2 = project_feasible(frac2, nn)
            h_b2, q_b2 = optimize_binary(frac2, nn, time.time() + min(rem * 0.45, 180.0), seed + 100 + 11 * idx)
            c_b2 = float(q_b2) * (2.0 / nn)
            if c_b2 < best_c:
                best_c = c_b2
                best_n = nn
                best_h = h_b2.astype(float).copy()

    if dl - time.time() > 70.0 and best_n <= 700:
        h_s, c_s = smooth_refine(best_h, best_n, dl - 4.0)
        if c_s < best_c:
            best_c = c_s
            best_h = h_s
            best_n = len(h_s)

    best_h = project_feasible(best_h, best_n)
    best_c = compute_c5(best_h, best_n)
    return best_h, float(best_c), int(best_n)
```
